I don't know. I just kind of stare at my supplies. Or I'll hear the phone ring and just ignore the calls from people. None of it feels like it's even worth starting anymore. I just don't get any enjoyment out of any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life during this difficult period?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I have. I've been having these thoughts lately that... well, that everyone would just be better off if I didn't wake up in the morning. And sometimes, especially when the back pain is really bad and the money worries just start piling up... I catch myself actually thinking about ending it. It honestly scares me that my mind goes there, but... yeah, those thoughts are there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything that isn't there. It's really just... my own thoughts getting too heavy, I guess. But no, no voices or visions or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I worry about money, like I said, and the bills piling up, but... it's not like I'm walking around feeling nervous or anxious all the time. It's more just this heavy, tired feeling. Not really worry, just... feeling like I can't fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you move around compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Everything I do is just slow now. I mean, even getting from the couch to the kitchen takes me longer than it should. It's not just walking, either... even answering a simple question just takes more time. People keep asking if I’m okay because... well, they say I move like I’m underwater.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so, mostly because my eating is just all over the place. I'll go most of the day just on coffee... food kind of turns my stomach lately. And then late at night, I'll just end up picking at whatever's in the cabinet, not really even tasting it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are not your own or that someone or something is controlling your mind?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. My thoughts are my own... they're just mostly negative lately. But no, I don't feel like anyone is controlling my mind or putting thoughts in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before in your life, where you felt this heavy and hopeless for an extended time?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I've had bad weeks before, or times where work was just really stressful, but... no, never anything like this. I've never felt this kind of heavy before, where it just doesn't go away.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood or energy levels change with the seasons?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. It's just... it's been pretty constant lately, regardless of the time of year or the weather outside.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode like this before that lasted for a while and then got better on its own or with treatment?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, I've never been through anything like this before. I've never needed treatment for my mood, and I haven't had anything like this just go away on its own, because... well, it's just never happened. This is a first for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt this low and hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying... this is the only time. I haven't had multiple periods of feeling like this in my life. Just this one that I'm dealing with right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

And sometimes, especially when the back pain is really bad and the money worries just start piling up... I catch myself actually thinking about ending it. It honestly scares me that my mind goes there, but... yeah, those thoughts are there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything that isn't there. It's really just... my own thoughts getting too heavy, I guess. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything that isn't there. It's really just... my own thoughts getting too heavy, I guess. But no, no voices or visions or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I worry about money, like I said, and the bills piling up, but... it's not like I'm walking around feeling nervous or anxious all the time. It's more just this heavy, tired feeling. Not really worry, just... feeling like I can't fix it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you move around compared to before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Everything I do is just slow now. I mean, even getting from the couch to the kitchen takes me longer than it should. It's not just walking, either... even answering a simple question just takes more time. People keep asking if I’m okay because... well, they say I move like I’m underwater.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so, mostly because my eating is just all over the place. I'll go most of the day just on coffee... food kind of turns my stomach lately. And then late at night, I'll just end up picking at whatever's in the cabinet, not really even tasting it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are not your own or that someone or something is controlling your mind?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. My thoughts are my own... they're just mostly negative lately. But no, I don't feel like anyone is controlling my mind or putting thoughts in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before in your life, where you felt this heavy and hopeless for an extended time?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I've had bad weeks before, or times where work was just really stressful, but... no, never anything like this. I've never felt this kind of heavy before, where it just doesn't go away.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood or energy levels change with the seasons?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. It's just... it's been pretty constant lately, regardless of the time of year or the weather outside.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode like this before that lasted for a while and then got better on its own or with treatment?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, I've never been through anything like this before. I've never needed treatment for my mood, and I haven't had anything like this just go away on its own, because... well, it's just never happened. This is a first for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt this low and hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying... this is the only time. I haven't had multiple periods of feeling like this in my life. Just this one that I'm dealing with right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in your life where you felt significantly better than you do now for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, before this all started, I always felt fine. Just normal, you know? But since I started feeling this low... no. It hasn't let up at all. I haven't had a few months where things went back to normal or felt significantly better. It's just been heavy like this the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in your life before this one where you felt similarly low and hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I was just saying a minute ago... I've never felt this low before in my whole life. This is the very first time I've ever dealt with anything like this.\n\nDoctor: Is this the first time in your life you've had a period of feeling this low and hopeless that's lasted for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes. Like I... I know I keep saying this, but yes. This is the first time. I've never had a time where I felt this bad before in my life, so... yeah, it's the first time it's lasted for more than two weeks, because it's the first time it's ever happened at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in your life where you felt significantly better than you do now for at least two months since this low and hopeless period started?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I mentioned a couple of minutes ago... since this whole thing started, it really hasn't let up at all. There hasn't been any kind of two-month break where I felt better. It's just been constant like this the entire time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this one where you felt low and hopeless for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: No... I really haven't. I'm sorry, I feel like we're going in circles a bit here, but... no. Like I just said, I have never felt this way before in my entire life. So there was never any two-week period before this one. This is the very first time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling normal or near-normal between episodes of feeling low and hopeless?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't. Um, since there haven't been different episodes, just this one long stretch... there hasn't been any time in between to feel normal. I haven't felt normal at all since this whole thing started. It's just been bad the entire time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
